Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  - Chronic Worry: Worry occurs more days than not for at least 6 months.\n  - Generalized Worry: Worry is not confined to specific topics but spreads to various domains like work, school, family, and finances.\n  - Catastrophizing: Jumping to conclusions that minor mistakes or events will lead to irreversible disasters.\n  - Intolerance of Uncertainty: Unable to tolerate future uncertainty, trapped in 'what if' questions.\n  - Failure to Control: Unable to regulate worry despite efforts to stop or distract oneself.\n  - Intrusive Worry: Worries suddenly intrude into the mind and interfere with focus while doing other tasks.\n  - Chaining of Worry: One worry triggers another in a chain reaction, escalating to catastrophic thoughts.\n  - Feeling Keyed Up: Feeling anxious and wound up like a spring, unable to stay still.\n  - Physical Tension: The entire body feels stiff and tense, unable to relax.\n  - Nervousness: Nerves are on edge, being startled easily by minor sounds or stimuli.\n  - Neck and Shoulder Tension: Muscles in the shoulders and back of the neck are knotted and stiff, causing pain or headaches.\n  - Jaw Tension: Clenching teeth or tension in the jaw causing jaw pain or headaches.\n  - Generalized Muscle Aches: Muscles all over the body, including back, arms, and legs, are stiff, painful, and easily fatigued.\n  - What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I've been feeling completely exhausted all the time, and I just can't seem to stop constantly worrying. I don't really have specific trouble sleeping, but I am always extremely fatigued and my body feels incredibly tense.\n\nDoctor: Do you also experience any other physical symptoms such as irritability, difficulty concentrating, or muscle tension, which are commonly associated with generalized anxiety disorder?\n\nPatient: Yes, I experience severe muscle tension all the time. My entire body feels incredibly stiff and unable to relax, with knotted muscles in my neck and shoulders, jaw tension, and general muscle aches all over. I also get mentally fatigued when trying to concentrate, but the constant physical stiffness is what is most noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any triggers or situations that tend to exacerbate your anxiety and worry, or does it seem to be more of a constant, underlying state for you?\n\nPatient: It definitely feels like a constant, underlying state that is almost always present rather than being triggered by specific situations. The worry just easily spreads to various everyday topics like my work, family, and finances, making it feel completely generalized and continuous."
  }
]
